### Open — contradictions on file (never auto-merged) (4)
- `338f5df70b2c` measurement **greets.displace.v4.tear** = 0 (hole_px) {arm=v4_p3 detector=v4_tear_cover pose=S_default_cam scene=greets state=P3} w=3/5 — The 42049 new-coverage px that 31 of the 54 tear poses report are ONE framing counted 31 times, and they are not a defect. Those poses carry cam - in docs/tears_poses.txt, which the battery replaces with the fixed 0,0,0,0,0,1 camera, so all 31 render the identical frame. At that framing the viewer looks ALONG the floor plane: the flat control draws the floor edge-on at zero height, and the displaced arm gives it relief, so its silhouette becomes a 39-px band at the horizon. Measured: all 42049 px are floor::mirUV, bounded to y in [540,578] across the full width, 40037 of them have all 8 neighbours rasterised in the ARM and 40129 have none rasterised in the CONTROL -- growth, not a tear -- and holes at that framing are 0.
- `87316669fbb8` measurement **greets.displace.v4.tear** = 14 (hole_px) {arm=v4_p3 detector=v4_tear_cover scene=greets state=P3} w=5/5 — P3 PASSES the tear cap at the shipped lattice: 14 hole px over 8 of 54 poses, against P2 s 15 over 9 and a 15 px cap. Control-referenced against --v4_flat on the same binary, tools/v4_tear_battery.sh + v4_tear_cover.py. Displacement s own contribution is ONE pixel (P5854 (1280,818) floor::mirUV, absent from the P2 arm at amp=0 and present at amp=0.3 with or without the lattice option); the other 13 are P2 debt reproduced at identical coordinates. Displacement also REMOVED one of P2 s (P5813 (960,864)). Provenance: the 54-pose battery was executed by the coordinator running this subagent s own chunk script while the subagent s stream was down; the data is /tmp/v4p3_tear.txt and /tmp/v4p3_tear_nbu.txt and the decomposition /tmp/v4p3_holedecomp.txt.
- `0a9d99d9ead8` measurement **platform.m5.shadow_plane_hash** = "pervasive_map_divergence" (state) {detector=sph pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — ANSWERED by his per-map M5 run (2026-08-31): the divergence is PERVASIVE, not localized - 41 of 48 StaticOnce maps, 26 of 28 DynOmnis maps and both content-bearing DynMeshes maps (53, 56) differ from the dev box; the few identical maps are the near-empty ones (hashes at/near the FNV basis signature). Every map with substantive rasterized content carries the LSB divergence, so map-level localization is EXHAUSTED and the parked +-1-ULP origin is confirmed to reach the shadow raster wholesale. The actionable lever is the AMPLIFIER: his m5_diag4 table shows --no-shadow_polyid alone restores the ceiling, i.e. the pure depth test reads LIT there - the polyId rescue is what hard-shadows a receiver when a CO-PLANAR different polygon owns the shadow-map texel (ownership flips freely under LSB noise). Robustness candidate: when polyIds differ but the stored depth is within a small guard band of the receiver depth (a co-planar occluder), treat as lit - byte-null to prove impossible (it changes a shadow decision), so gate: default-off flag, dev-box pins byte-identical with flag off, his M5 run + his eye judge the flag-on arm.
- `149c89a479b3` measurement **platform.m5.shadow_plane_hash** = "sph_differs" (state) {detector=sph pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — ANSWERED by his M5 run (2026-08-31): sph_differs - all three [SPH] invocations differ from the dev box, INCLUDING seq=0 StaticOnce (the one-shot deterministic static bake, 48 maps, 12.58M texels). The shadow MAPS themselves are machine-dependent at the packed depth+polyId plane level, so the divergence is inside the shadow RASTER, not the lookup; the ceiling defect is the polyId rescue failing against a map whose texels a different (co-planar) polygon owns on the M5. Next: per-map hashes to name WHICH light/face flips, then a texel-level plane diff for the mechanism.

### Not authoritative — on a judged subject without human provenance (3)

Needed: arm attribution and one pose.

- `032cf9150e6a` measurement **greets.displace.ref.miss_cause** = 100.0 (share_pct) {arm=ref defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_mitre_trim metric=miss_px_vetoed_by_trim pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5 — Every hole the reference renderer leaves is the MITRE TRIM applied where the survey rule does not reach. At cam A 4007 of 4009 miss pixels were vetoed by MitreTrimOk and NOT ONE of those vetoes came from a convex edge (1717 concave, 2290 soup-neighbour); at corridor t=5534, 8820 of 8946 with only 92 convex. The survey sentence is a CONVEX-corner rule; applied at every shared edge it turns the model union into an intersection, and against a SOUP neighbour it compares the point to the neighbour bare authored plane because there is no height field there.

- `e3e93ecd9546` measurement **greets.displace.v4.junctions.coplanar_share** = 1227.7179 (world_units) {defect=wall flag=greets_displace_v4 metric=coplanar_junction_len scene=greets} w=3/5 — 64.5% of ALL stone chart-junction length is SAME-PLANE seams: [V4-CHARTS] planepair planes=52 junctions=142 total_len=1904.1782 noncoplanar_junctions=96 noncoplanar_len=676.4603 coplanar_edges=157 coplanar_len=1227.7179 planes_with_multiple_charts=8 max_charts_per_plane=6. - `938586f97705` measurement **greets.displace.v4.junctions.reconciliation** = 142 (count) {flag=greets_displace_v4 metric=plane_pair_junctions scene=greets state=P1} w=4/5 — recheck of 9a42678e68cf: The crease census's 453 plane-pair junctions and 7375.2 u (83aa7c0b6c4c) count the MIRROR-CLONED render-time scene, not the bake set. Measured both ways on one binary: default (mirrors on) reproduces 453 junctions / 7375.2 u over 892 authored stone faces exactly; --no-greets_mirror gives 142 junctions / 1904.2 u over 226 stone faces. The v4 bake-time census, over the same 226 faces at the same point the old bake runs and using the reference's own plane clustering, reads 142 plane-pair junctions / 1904.1782 u -- the same count and the same length to 1e-4 u, an independent second implementation agreeing with the reference. The 4x is GreetsMirror cloning the room geometry for the teleporter and the three P_TEXT mirrors AFTER the bake; the ratio is not exactly 4 (453 vs 4x142) because the clones are mirror-bbox-clipped partial copies and the plane clustering is a greedy 0.5 deg / 5 mm first-match. The bake, old and v4, sees the 226 and the clone build copies its result -- so the junction population v4's corner rule must serve is 142, not 453. — 'tools/v4_p1_gate.sh --json | python3 -c \'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)["planepair_junctions"])\'' -> 142 (was 142, agrees)

- `81d16274bfd0` measurement **greets.displace.v4.p3_plateau_displacement** = "built-gate-not-met" (state) {defect=wall flag=greets_displace_v4 scene=greets state=P3} w=5/5 — P3 IS BUILT AND ITS GATE IS NOT MET. The height rule shipped exactly as proposed -- interior nodes along the CHART PLANE NORMAL (max chart-vs-face deviation 1e-06 deg), plateau heights from the max-pyramid, groove from the min, bevel bilinear, all 1649 authored-edge vertices pinned at 0 and 28946 interior nodes moved, d in [-0.1641, +0.0308]. Of the two end conditions: nspace_relief plateau bias FAILS (e-r p50 -0.0556 u, core -0.1065, 33 of 33 planes outside +-0.005) and refrender dz p50 at block interiors FAILS 4 of 4 poses at 2.91-4.84x the bare floor against a 2x bar. Gates that PASS: OFF arm byte-identical on all four rows, v4 arm 24/24 deterministic, tear 14 px of a 15 cap, slivers p10 2.41 (P2 2.26) with the same 8 sub-1deg faces, faces unchanged at 60606, bake 55.8 ms of a 500 ms budget. The cause is NOT the height rule and NOT the pinned rings: at --v4_cpb=1 the block interior carries no lattice node at all (mechanism 256d9f38b114), and the same height rule with a node in the block reads plateau -0.0084 and dz 0.74-0.78x the bare floor, PASSING the dz condition 4 of 4. What blocks the phase is the LATTICE, which is P2 s stage (c) and P7 s density dial, and the one lattice change measured here (--v4_band_union) opens 5 interior pinholes and breaches the face budget (d5b5dbb0f1d4). P4 must not start on this: the plateau the corner rule reconciles is 0.1 u out of place.
- `256d9f38b114` measurement **greets.displace.v4.relief.mechanism** = -0.0799 (world_units) {arm=v4_p3 defect=block_interior_unresolved metric=plateau_emr_p50_grid_plateau scene=greets state=P3} w=3/5 — WHY the P3 relief recesses, localized to one mechanism rather than to the height rule. The wall is a RUNNING BOND: band k+1 s vertical mortar sits at the middle of band k s blocks. At --v4_cpb=1 the target cell IS the block pitch, so ceil(pitch/target)=1 and a block interior carries NO column line of its own -- the [V4-DISPLACE] census reads 8638 plateau nodes against 20308 groove nodes, and every one of them sits on a mortar pad line. The only nodes inside a band-k block come from the union ROW at a band change, where band k+1 s mortar columns are injected, and the row below has nothing under them; the Delaunay then covers the block with triangles whose three corners are ALL mortar-deep. Measured directly at cam A: a triangle at (153.25,127.5) (150.75,127.5) (126.75,189.75) texels with corner heights h=0.048/0.027/0.233, spanning a 57.5-texel block whose field reads h=0.59. Cross-tabulating the field class against the geometric class, the plateau-by-field / plateau-by-grid cell holds 149571 samples at e-r p50 -0.0799 u. This also settles a design premise: 2c s restricted-quadtree rule (adjacent cells differ by <=1 level) is NOT satisfied by the P2 lattice -- a 2.5-texel mortar cell abuts a 57.5-texel block cell, 4.5 levels apart -- and P2 s level_jump_violations=0 measured the row SPLIT COUNT, not the cell sizes.

- `32d60f40a504` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band** = 61.0 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pct_of_1920x1080_frame_inside_the_256px_border_band pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — Because srad is pinned at its 256-px cap, the band over which the GTAO march loses samples off the frame is 256 FULL-RES PX WIDE on every side - 61 % of a 1920x1080 frame. Whole-frame effect at cam A: meanAO 0.7795 -> 0.8406 at inset 128 and 0.8995 at inset 256.
- `0d44df27177b` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band.asymmetry** = -0.2846 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_edge_inset_side metric=topleft_minus_bottomright_total_border_ao_loss pose=t5965-and-t5534 scene=greets} w=5/5 — RE-TESTED ON HIS EXACT VIEW CLASS AND IT STILL IS NOT TOP/LEFT - IF ANYTHING IT IS BOTTOM/RIGHT. Four content-matched probes (--ssao_edge_inset_side, the SAME pixels with a synthetic border 32 px to each side), including the corridor pose t=5534 whose LEFT WALL is grazing (|N.V| 0.27-0.47) and whose CEILING near the top of the frame is near-parallel (|N.V| 0.122) - the class the observation named. AO lift L/R/T/B: pose A centre 0.063/0.104/0.078/0.078; pose A top-left grazing wall (320,200) 0.043/0.197/0.152/0.075; corridor left wall (320,200) 0.027/0.015/0.001/0.090; corridor grazing ceiling (700,120) 0.027/0.051/0.016/0.044. The worst side tracks WHERE THE OCCLUDERS ARE, not the screen axis. The decisive form of the question - a corner is only ever exposed to ITS two borders - MIRRORED-CORNER TEST at identical inset magnitudes (288 px horizontal, 168 px vertical): pose A top-left loses 0.1942 AO total vs BOTTOM-RIGHT 0.4788; corridor top-left 0.0277 vs bottom-right 0.2977. In visible 8-bit levels on the lit frame the same ordering holds (A: 17.2 vs 36.8; corridor: 2.2 vs 33.5), and the bottom-right corner is the DARKER of the two (luma 49/63 vs 98/123), so it is not hidden by low contrast either.
- `c7e54ee64e11` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band.asymmetry** = -0.0402 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_edge_inset_side metric=ao_lift_left_minus_right_at_k32 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE BORDER INFORMATION LOSS ITSELF IS NOT TOP/LEFT-BIASED. Content-matched test (--ssao_edge_inset_side puts a synthetic border k px to the LEFT of a chosen pixel in one run and k px to its RIGHT in another, so the SAME pixels are compared): at the frame centre column/row of cam A the AO lift is LEFT +0.0633 vs RIGHT +0.1035 at k=32 (right WORSE) and TOP +0.0775 vs BOTTOM +0.0784 (equal). At k=128: LEFT +0.0159 / RIGHT +0.0581, TOP +0.0124 / BOTTOM +0.0300. So which side reads worst is set by where the occluders happen to lie, not by the algorithm - the only mechanism with a genuine top/left sign is the march-offset truncation (see greets.ssao.gtao.round_truncation), and that one is two orders of magnitude smaller.
- `a0fefb201622` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.resolution_dependence** = 12.6 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pp_swing_in_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — GTAO AO IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE SCENE - it moves with the pixel grid, and the hemisphere sampler does not. Same camera, same geometry, same flags, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed: GTAO ao<0.9 = 73.7 % at 1280x720, 66.5 % at 1920x1080, 61.1 % at 2560x1440 (meanAO 0.7732 / 0.7795 / 0.7928) - a 12.6-POINT swing from resolution alone. The --no-ssao_gtao hemisphere control at the same two of those resolutions gives 44.4 % and 44.3 % (meanAO 0.8778 / 0.8776), i.e. FLAT. Mechanism: the 256-px cap binds, so the world AO radius is cap*viewZ/FOVX, and FOVX is proportional to XRes - the radius therefore scales with depth AND inversely with horizontal resolution. This is the view-dependent shading Gil-Ad reported, and it is why his ladder cleared --no-ssao_gtao.
- `1a94791af617` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.round_fix.frame_ms** = -0.08 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_round_fix metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta pose=t5743 scene=greets} w=5/5 — --ssao_gtao_round_fix is PERF-NEUTRAL-TO-SLIGHTLY-CHEAPER, as its instruction count predicts (one _mm256_cvtps_epi32 replaces an _mm256_add_ps plus an _mm256_cvttps_epi32, per axis per sample). greets t=5743, 1920x1080, --deferred_prof=5 --bench iters=40, 11 INTERLEAVED runs per arm on ONE binary: renderFrame wall_min 45.845 ON vs 45.880 OFF (-0.08 %); the ssao wave 6.964 vs 7.038 (-1.05 %); ssao-march 4.895 vs 4.941 (-0.93 %). A separate earlier 11x11 battery gave renderFrame -0.299 ms (-0.64 %). Every figure is INSIDE the +/-1.5-2 % placement floor, so the claim is neutrality, not a win - but the sign is the same in both batteries and in both the min and the median.
- `05d9bc72538a` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.round_fix.gates** = "byte-null-off-4-ssao-pins-move" (state) {arm=pin-battery-differential flag=ssao_gtao_round_fix metric=gate_outcome scene=greets} w=5/5 — GATE RESULT for --ssao_gtao_round_fix default ON, NOT re-baselined (that is the owner call). --no-ssao_gtao_round_fix is BYTE-EXACT to the pre-fix binary (AO plane and colour PPM both cmp-identical at cam A). THE STANDING PINS ARE INTACT: the same binary with --no-ssao_gtao_round_fix reproduces all four 2026-08-16y values EXACTLY (440aa6bbb350ae95fbacf339dd2ad957 / 00d17bc5610624bd1fea698c4b096979 / 29c1e7fbd30e9ef811588a63d0778b7b / bc1b0a8a703d6d6f6b3953eafc864d48), so the flag off is byte-null against the countersigned baseline, not merely against another build of itself.
- `9f6575e68c03` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.round_truncation** = 0.0044 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_round_fix metric=rms_ao_delta_of_the_fix pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — REAL BUG, TOP/LEFT-SIGNED, SECOND ORDER. The GTAO march computed its sample pixel as `px + int(off + 0.5f)`, and C truncation toward zero makes that round-half-up ONLY for off >= -0.5; every negative offset lands ONE PIXEL SHORT. It does not average out over the rotations, because buildSliceTrig builds phi = (s + jit)*(PI/slices) with jit in [0,1], so phi never leaves [0,PI) and sin(phi) - the y component of every slice direction at all 16 rotations - is non-negative: every UP-going sample landed 1 px low and every LEFT-going sample 1 px right, while down/right were exact. Fixed by rounding to nearest (--ssao_gtao_round_fix, default ON, one instruction CHEAPER). Delta at cam A: rms 0.0044 AO, max 0.0908, 3.39 % of px move >0.01; asymmetric in the predicted direction - top 256-px band mean -0.00185 (6.82 % of px >0.01) vs bottom +0.00186 (1.12 %), left -0.00140 (4.24 %) vs right -0.00068 (0.03 %).
- `bfd122c99a15` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.srad_clamp** = 99.99 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pct_covered_px_where_cap_binds pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=3/5 — The GTAO march hard 256-px screen-radius cap (FDS/RENDER/DeferredSSAO.cpp#gtaoCell / #gtaoRow8: srad = ssao_radius*FOVX/z clamped to [2,256]) BINDS on 92-100 % of covered pixels at the greets review poses, so the AO scale in this scene is set by the cap, not by --ssao_radius. From the --ssao_dump view-Z plane at 1920x1080 (FOVX=1728, --ssao_radius=4, the zfloor inert in greets) the unclamped srad median is 894 px at t=5965, 1034 at t=5743 and 9333 at t=6097, and the cap binds on 99.99 / 92.12 / 100.00 % of covered pixels.
- `5f95e8304799` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.edge_band** = -53.0 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=pct_change_in_border_ao_error_0_to_32px pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — The front-loaded ladder roughly HALVES the border error in the strip where he said it is most jarring, and pays a little for it further in. Content-matched (--ssao_edge_inset=256, cam A 1920x1080), mean AO error by distance inward from the synthetic border, averaged over all four sides: 0-16 px +0.0716 -> +0.0404 (-44 %), 0-32 px +0.0640 -> +0.0299 (-53 %), 0-64 px +0.0438 -> +0.0225 (-49 %), 64-128 px +0.0105 -> +0.0118, 128-256 px +0.0036 -> +0.0058. The band-vs-interior gap on the untouched frame goes +0.0226 (uniform/256) to -0.0015 (geometric/1024) at pose A. CAVEAT STATED BECAUSE IT MATTERS: a 256-px inset fully accounts for a 256-px march but only partly for a ~894-px one, so the geometric arm's TOTAL border sensitivity is UNDER-measured by this instrument; the near-edge profile is the comparable statistic, the whole-frame mean is not (0.1200 vs 0.1249 is instrument, not physics).
- `846db4d1125f` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.frame_ms** = 0.12 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta pose=t5743 scene=greets} w=5/5 — COST-NEUTRAL, and the SSAO wave itself is slightly CHEAPER. greets t=5743, 1920x1080, --deferred_prof=5 --bench iters=40, 11 INTERLEAVED runs per arm on ONE binary, wall_min: renderFrame 49.792 (uniform/256) vs 49.853 (geometric/1024), +0.061 ms = +0.12 %, inside the +/-1.5-2 % placement floor; the ssao wave 7.140 -> 6.853 (-4.02 %) and ssao-march 5.007 -> 4.728 (-5.57 %), with the medians agreeing in sign (renderFrame median is LOWER for the geometric arm, 50.336 vs 51.482). Mechanism for the march being cheaper rather than dearer: the step count is unchanged, but a ~894-px march puts more samples OFF-FRAME, and the off-frame path skips the depth gather, both gtaoAcos calls and the bitmask build - and a whole 8-lane batch with no live sample takes the `if (!any) continue` exit. The per-cell ladder constants cost two quadratic minimax evaluations, not libm.
- `e279eac2ea39` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.near_step_artifacts** = 0.03 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024 flag=ssao_gtao_thick_clamp metric=pct_fully_black_px_in_the_saw_tooth_column pose=t5726 scene=greets} w=5/5 — BOTH OF HIS ARTIFACTS ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS NOT A DEPTH-DISCONTINUITY BUG. Traced at t=5726 px(1129,225): the black saw-tooth column is NOT the near wall occluding the far background - those samples are correctly rejected (OUT-OF-RANGE, |d| = 31.5 against a 4 u radius). The black pixels are ON THE NEAR WALL, grazing, just inside its own silhouette, occluding THEMSELVES. Two guards were missing because the uniform ladder put the first sample 48-192 px out and never needed them. (a) THICKNESS: the bitmask back face is d - V*thickness, a flat world constant, so when |d| is much smaller than thickness it lands BEHIND the receiver and acos(dot(b,V)) saturates at PI - in the trace one sample at |d| = 0.066 set 11 of 32 sectors and one at 0.285 set THIRTY, and the saturation vanishes at the first step whose |d| (1.274) exceeds thickness (1.0). Fixed by --ssao_gtao_thick_clamp: back face bounded by min(thickness,|d|). (b) SELF-OCCLUSION: --ssao_bias is read ONLY by the hemisphere sampler, so GTAO shipped with no bias at all; at ~5 px the ZPage16 quantum (0.0025 u) and the half-res cell offset become a horizon at grazing incidence. Fixed by --ssao_gtao_bias (plane distance, AUTO 0.05 u). RESULTS at 1512x848 on his exact arm: t=5726 column fully-black fraction 32.90 % -> 0.03 % (std 0.343 -> 0.216); t=5836 column 8.23 % -> 0.00 % (std 0.280 -> 0.148, the shipped uniform arm is 0.147); t=5836 near-wall banding std 0.1462 -> 0.0725, BELOW the shipped uniform arm's 0.0796. Isolation: bias alone leaves 11.58 %/1.36 % black (the clamp owns the columns), clamp alone leaves the banding at std 0.139 (the bias owns the banding). --ssao_downscale=1 changes neither (37.59 %/9.86 % black), so the bilateral upsample is NOT implicated - that hypothesis is refuted.
- `6c0f5448991c` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.resolution** = 0.47 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=pp_spread_of_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560_at_cap4096 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE STRUCTURAL FIX WORKS: front-loaded march steps plus a cap large enough to stop binding collapse GTAO's resolution dependence to the hemisphere sampler's level. Same camera, same geometry, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed; ao<0.9 spread over 1280x720 / 1920x1080 / 2560x1440. Pose A: uniform+cap256 12.4 pp -> geometric+cap512 3.5 -> +cap1024 3.3 -> +cap4096 (cap binds 0.00 %) 0.47 pp, against the hemisphere control's 0.2 pp. Pose B: 8.6 -> 5.1 -> 1.7 -> 0.21 pp, hemisphere 0.5. meanAO at pose A goes 0.7729/0.7793/0.7925 (spread 0.0196) to 0.7780/0.7800/0.7821 (spread 0.0041). Pose C does NOT converge (5.9 -> 5.2 pp at cap 1024, 5.6 at cap 4096) and it is not a failure of the lever: at view-Z median 0.74 the authored 4-unit radius subtends more than the whole frame, the cap still binds on 84-99 % of pixels, and no screen-space method can carry that radius there. GEOMETRIC SPACING IS WHAT MAKES THE RAISED CAP USABLE: at cap 1024 the four steps land at 5.1/26.1/133.2/681.0 px instead of uniform's 192/448/704/960, and uniform at the same cap costs +0.0290 AO of interior contact occlusion where geometric costs +0.0152.
- `ab38cdd47b7e` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.resolution** = 0.0059 (world_units) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024-guards flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=meanAO_spread_over_1280_to_2560 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE GUARDS DO NOT REGRESS THE RESOLUTION RESULT - THEY EXTEND IT TO THE ONE POSE THAT HAD REFUSED TO CONVERGE. meanAO spread over 1280x720 / 1920x1080 / 2560x1440 with the guards on: pose A 0.0059, pose B 0.0030, pose C 0.0010, against the shipped uniform arm's 0.0196 / 0.0192 / 0.0019 and the unguarded geometric arm's 0.0017 / 0.0046 / 0.0556. Pose C is the win: its self-occlusion was itself resolution-dependent, so removing it collapses a 0.0556 spread to 0.0010. ao<0.9 spreads are 2.6 / 4.5 / 0.8 pp against the shipped 12.4 / 8.6 / 5.9. - `d585fec1a494` measurement **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.causal_ab** = "83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281" (snapshot_md5) {arm=idshift_unguarded defect=ceiling_colour pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=2/5 — THE M5 CEILING REPRODUCED ON A CORRECT MACHINE, AND THE GUARD RESTORES IT. greets cam A t=5965, 1920x1080, dev box, clean Release at 81c4db52. --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 stamps id+N into the shadow map's id half leaving the DEPTH half untouched, so every receiver's own texels carry a non-matching id at ZERO depth separation - the M5's ceiling state, frame-wide. THIS RECORD'S VALUE IS THE UNGUARDED REPRO SNAPSHOT md5, 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281, whose ceiling band (rows 5..40, cols 600..1300) reads 82.8/63.4/47.6 against the M5's measured 85.3/65.8/47.8. The other three arms of the same battery: shipping d92cb6f5eb19da4301588ae83af6a56e = 85.2/133.5/139.0; idshift + --shadow_coplanar_guard 8dbbafb77629598e5a3e2b1bfaae9e1a = 85.1/133.8/139.3, i.e. with EVERY identity rescue in the scene destroyed the guard alone carries the frame back to within 0.3/255 of the shipping look; idshift + guard + --shadow_coplanar_guard_onesided bc7a68cc2e5eeec1ca4ab8ef350b5658, which is --no-shadow_polyid BYTE FOR BYTE (so requiring the depth test to agree degenerates to depth mode and the SYMMETRIC band is the actual guard). The raster-noise lever --shadow_coplanar_flip=K (last writer wins within K quanta) is much weaker: K=1 moves 3 px, K<=8 moves at most 406 px in one 250x160 box and NEVER the ceiling band - exact and near-exact ownership ties are rare in these maps, so tie-flipping alone cannot be the whole M5 story; the M5 needs the id half itself to differ, which its pervasive map divergence supplies.

- `dad4de8f8962` measurement **platform.m5.polyid_has_no_depth_term** = "identity_only_no_bias" (state) {defect=ceiling_colour scene=greets state=mechanism_2026_08_31 tool=m5} w=1/5 — MECHANISM CONFIRMED FROM THE CODE (docs/SHADOW_GUARD.md §1): ShadowMode::PolyId decides occlusion by IDENTITY ALONE - `occ += w iff c != 0 && c != receiverId` in CubeShadow_Tail (FDS/FILLERS/ShadowMap.h), with NO depth term and NO bias; DeferredShadowSampling.h passes constBias=0/slopeBias=0 into it on purpose. Depth mode's constBias(512)+slopeBias(<=4096) IS the acne rescue and it is OWNERSHIP-BLIND, so a co-planar occluder reads LIT whoever wrote the texel - which is exactly why --no-shadow_polyid restores the M5 ceiling while the default shadows it. PolyId's only rescue is id equality, so a different-shadow-group co-planar neighbour hard-shadows the receiver with ZERO depth separation from its own plane. Ownership is decided by `enc > z_existing` in ShadowBarry, i.e. by ONE quantum, and the M5's maps diverge on 41/48 static maps. SHARPENING of the parked hypothesis: the id is a MATERIAL/shadow-group id (Material::ShadowMatID, else ID+1), NOT a per-polygon id, so same-group co-planar polygons never fight - and greets allocates 2183 per-plane-cluster shadow groups by design ([PIRAMID-SPLIT] census), which is 2183 chances for the mismatch. The predicate is copied in FOUR places: the cube tail, LightmapBake.cpp SampleStaticCubeAtWorld (the path greets STATIC omnis actually take), the spot tap in DeferredSurfaceKernel.cpp, and the cached tail in CubeTapPrepass.h.

Every map with substantive rasterized content carries the LSB divergence, so map-level localization is EXHAUSTED and the parked +-1-ULP origin is confirmed to reach the shadow raster wholesale. The actionable lever is the AMPLIFIER: his m5_diag4 table shows --no-shadow_polyid alone restores the ceiling, i.e. the pure depth test reads LIT there - the polyId rescue is what hard-shadows a receiver when a CO-PLANAR different polygon owns the shadow-map texel (ownership flips freely under LSB noise). Robustness candidate: when polyIds differ but the stored depth is within a small guard band of the receiver depth (a co-planar occluder), treat as lit - byte-null to prove impossible (it changes a shadow decision), so gate: default-off flag, dev-box pins byte-identical with flag off, his M5 run + his eye judge the flag-on arm.

### Floating premises — laws/traps a current record rests on, that rest on no measurement (4)
- `420fcd4626b8` law **displace.law.direction_and_shading_separate** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5 — The displacement direction is a separate field from the shading normal: on a planar patch it is the face normal (nothing to pollute); the shading normal is n ~ N - grad_s h in the plane frame, exact on a plane, with split normals at creases (dihedral threshold) and never an average across a crease.
- `f713599ea11d` law **displace.law.mitre_offset_planes** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5 — At a crease, displace the crease point by solving the two offset planes, u = d(n1+n2)/(1+n1.n2) (3x3 with the edge direction for unequal offsets) - never a normalised bisector. Sign-free at reflex corners; the only degeneracy is anti-parallel outward normals (a razor fin, material dihedral -> 0), handled by a mitre limit (SVG default 4). Every corner in greets caps at 2x the height.
- `7453a529070e` law **displace.law.tjunctions_before_displacement** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5 — T-junctions and duplicate border vertices must be eliminated BEFORE displacement: a shared-border vertex is evaluated once and indexed from both sides (R1), the border owns its sample count (R2), border parameters are exact i/n (R3). Displacing a T-vertex by its own sampled height turns a sub-pixel raster crack into a full-amplitude hole.
- `e43c035dbedf` trap **greets.displace.ref.bisector_partition_deletes_material** = "trap" (state) {defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_partition pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5 — The LITERAL candidate definition - the nearest base plane governing MEMBERSHIP - does not merely castellate, it deletes material: wherever the governing face height says air while a neighbour slab says solid, the point is air. A plane bisector is a NON-LOCAL partition, so a face seen edge-on has its plane nearest along an entire ray. Traced at cam A t=5965 px(1240,300): edge-on face 881 governs from t=5.58 while the pier own front faces begin at t=5.90, deleting the pier front surface over a 90 px band. The survey records that no source defines the bisector-partitioned union.

- `c868b8e20c97` measurement **chase.ssao.gtao.step_dist.frame_ms** = 0.02 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-stepdist1-srad1024-guards-bias02 flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=renderFrame_main_wall_min_pct_delta_vs_preround_default pose=t800 scene=chase} w=5/5 — 11 INTERLEAVED --bench pairs on ONE binary (arms differ only by flags: the OLD arm is --ssao_gtao_step_dist=0 --ssao_gtao_srad_max=256 --no-ssao_gtao_round_fix, proved byte-exact against the pre-round build), iters=40, 1920x1080 forced, --deferred_prof=5 --profiler=0, dummy drivers, wall_min. CHASE PAYS NOTHING FOR THE FLIP, AND ITS SSAO ROWS GET CHEAPER. chase renders TWO tagged frames per tick and both are flat: renderFrame@main 19.630 -> 19.634 ms (+0.02 %), renderFrame@refl 16.601 -> 16.649 (+0.29 %) - both an order of magnitude inside the +/-1.5-2 % floor. The SSAO rows move the OPPOSITE way to greets: ssao@main -0.99 %, ssao@refl -1.72 %, ssao-march@main -1.77 %, ssao-march@refl -2.67 %, ssao-blur -2.5/-3.3 %. Mechanism consistent with the greets record: with the cap at 1024 the march is ~4x longer in screen space, so far more samples land OFF-FRAME, and an off-frame sample skips the depth gather, both gtaoAcos calls and the bitmask build (a whole 8-lane batch with no live sample takes the `if (!any) continue` exit). In greets' tight interior that saving is outweighed by the guards; in chase's open corridor it wins.

### displace
- `420fcd4626b8` law **displace.law.direction_and_shading_separate** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating floating_premise] — The displacement direction is a separate field from the shading normal: on a planar patch it is the face normal (nothing to pollute); the shading normal is n ~ N - grad_s h in the plane frame, exact on a plane, with split normals at creases (dihedral threshold) and never an average across a crease.
- `f713599ea11d` law **displace.law.mitre_offset_planes** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating floating_premise] — At a crease, displace the crease point by solving the two offset planes, u = d(n1+n2)/(1+n1.n2) (3x3 with the edge direction for unequal offsets) - never a normalised bisector. Sign-free at reflex corners; the only degeneracy is anti-parallel outward normals (a razor fin, material dihedral -> 0), handled by a mitre limit (SVG default 4). Every corner in greets caps at 2x the height.
- `7453a529070e` law **displace.law.tjunctions_before_displacement** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating floating_premise] — T-junctions and duplicate border vertices must be eliminated BEFORE displacement: a shared-border vertex is evaluated once and indexed from both sides (R1), the border owns its sample count (R2), border parameters are exact i/n (R3). Displacing a T-vertex by its own sampled height turns a sub-pixel raster crack into a full-amplitude hole.

- `5452fa7984d7` measurement **flag.shadow_polyid.cli_effective** = "cli_effective" (state) {flag=shadow_polyid pose=conetest recipe=render_gate} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — --no-shadow_polyid ON THE COMMAND LINE now takes effect; before b46ba7b5 it was INERT. g_shadowMode was a namespace-scope DYNAMIC initialiser reading FeatureFlags::shadow_polyid(), so it ran before main() parsed argv — the tree's only ERROR-severity semgrep finding (revival-featureflags-at-namespace-scope). Measured on the gate's own conetest recipe at 1920x1080 (--deferred --draw_cones --shadows --fast_fog --fast_fog_density=3): BEFORE, default and --no-shadow_polyid were both b41894f969d1f89dd2d7d794f160e286, bit for bit, while FDS_SHADOW_POLYID=0 gave be71c7ab18f23573daa2d1b9badc06fc. AFTER, default and --shadow_polyid stay b41894f969d1f89dd2d7d794f160e286 and --no-shadow_polyid gives be71c7ab18f23573daa2d1b9badc06fc, i.e. exactly what the env form always gave. Fix = a constant initialiser off FDS_SHADOW_POLYID_DEFAULT_ON plus one explicit Shadow_SeedModeFromFlags() called from REV.CPP right after FeatureFlags::parseArgs; NOT a lazy read, because g_shadowMode is loaded per shadow tap in DeferredSurfaceKernel. Default arm byte-null: render_gate ALL PASS 4/4, warm_gate fast ALL PASS, greets cam A t=5965 still dbe2d4c2716e2d762b366d5a45c151a6. tools/lint.sh after the fix: revival-featureflags-at-namespace-scope 0 findings, 0 at ERROR severity (the 102 raw-getenv WARNINGs are untouched), flag_readers 613/613 OK.

- `e43c035dbedf` trap **greets.displace.ref.bisector_partition_deletes_material** = "trap" (state) {defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_partition pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5  [floating floating_premise] — The LITERAL candidate definition - the nearest base plane governing MEMBERSHIP - does not merely castellate, it deletes material: wherever the governing face height says air while a neighbour slab says solid, the point is air. Traced at cam A t=5965 px(1240,300): edge-on face 881 governs from t=5.58 while the pier own front faces begin at t=5.90, deleting the pier front surface over a 90 px band. The survey records that no source defines the bisector-partitioned union.

- `032cf9150e6a` measurement **greets.displace.ref.miss_cause** = 100.0 (share_pct) {arm=ref defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_mitre_trim metric=miss_px_vetoed_by_trim pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — Every hole the reference renderer leaves is the MITRE TRIM applied where the survey rule does not reach. At cam A 4007 of 4009 miss pixels were vetoed by MitreTrimOk and NOT ONE of those vetoes came from a convex edge (1717 concave, 2290 soup-neighbour); at corridor t=5534, 8820 of 8946 with only 92 convex. The survey sentence is a CONVEX-corner rule; applied at every shared edge it turns the model union into an intersection, and against a SOUP neighbour it compares the point to the neighbour bare authored plane because there is no height field there.

- `60e3e63bed65` trap **greets.displace.ref.smooth_seam_margin** = "trap" (state) {defect=wall flag=greets_displace_ref_margin scene=greets tool=DisplaceStoneSubdiv} w=3/5  [floating] — cot(phi/2) is the BISECTOR partition requirement, not every edge requirement. Sizing a SMOOTH seam by it (cot 5 deg = 11.4) replaced the curved wall with its tangent planes extended a whole unit, each strip standing proud of the curve by the sagitta and the pair mutually satisfying each other mitre trim. A crease is a corner and needs max(cot,tan)(phi/2); a smooth seam is one surface and needs only the mitre tan(phi/2). Max margin 1.059 -> 0.578 u.

- `256d9f38b114` measurement **greets.displace.v4.relief.mechanism** = -0.0799 (world_units) {arm=v4_p3 defect=block_interior_unresolved metric=plateau_emr_p50_grid_plateau scene=greets state=P3} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — WHY the P3 relief recesses, localized to one mechanism rather than to the height rule. The wall is a RUNNING BOND: band k+1 s vertical mortar sits at the middle of band k s blocks. At --v4_cpb=1 the target cell IS the block pitch, so ceil(pitch/target)=1 and a block interior carries NO column line of its own -- the [V4-DISPLACE] census reads 8638 plateau nodes against 20308 groove nodes, and every one of them sits on a mortar pad line. The only nodes inside a band-k block come from the union ROW at a band change, where band k+1 s mortar columns are injected, and the row below has nothing under them; the Delaunay then covers the block with triangles whose three corners are ALL mortar-deep. Measured directly at cam A: a triangle at (153.25,127.5) (150.75,127.5) (126.75,189.75) texels with corner heights h=0.048/0.027/0.233, spanning a 57.5-texel block whose field reads h=0.59. Cross-tabulating the field class against the geometric class, the plateau-by-field / plateau-by-grid cell holds 149571 samples at e-r p50 -0.0799 u. This also settles a design premise: 2c s restricted-quadtree rule (adjacent cells differ by <=1 level) is NOT satisfied by the P2 lattice -- a 2.5-texel mortar cell abuts a 57.5-texel block cell, 4.5 levels apart -- and P2 s level_jump_violations=0 measured the row SPLIT COUNT, not the cell sizes.

Mechanism consistent with the union placing two column lines from DIFFERENT bands within a texel of each other on one row, where the relief class flips between them and the surface takes a 0.16 u step across ~1 texel; kLineMergeTex is 1.0 texel and does not see the class. - **greets.displace.v4.undisplaced_byte_identity** — 2 current records, by arm; no single current value
    - `aab5e6484587` arm=old_bake_amp0: 50.731 (share_pct) — The control measurement of the same quantity on the SHIPPED bake: DisplaceStoneSubdiv at --greets_displace_amp=0 against the --v4_flat control differs on 50.73 percent of cam A's pixels, max channel delta 197. Recorded as its own row so the v4 number has a yardstick rather than a target.
    - `d93da1ef614c` arm=v4_lattice: 48.9067 (share_pct) — DESIGN SECTION 2c IS WRONG: an undisplaced retessellation of the greets stone CANNOT render byte-identically to the flat wall in this engine, and the reason has nothing to do with v4. The albedo mip is chosen PER CLIPPED POLYGON (FDS/FRUSTRUM/FRUSTRUM.CPP:945 -- below MinSize = XRes*YRes*0.02 one mip is taken for the whole polygon, above it the mipmap-via-subdivision path splits it), so any change to the stone's tessellation moves which mip a pixel samples: 265108 pixels change mip level at cam A between the v4 lattice and the --v4_flat control (the SAME pipeline emitting the 226 authored triangles), which is what the 48.91 percent colour difference is. Put the SHIPPED bake at --greets_displace_amp=0 through the identical comparison and it is slightly worse: 50.73 percent, z16 differing 3149 (3145 of them by one quantum) against v4's 2828 (2826), 3 raster-vs-empty flips against 1, 6 material-ownership flips (4 against a non-stone sheet) against 5 (3), same max channel delta 197. Over cam A plus the 18 review poses: v4 reaches tier 2 cleanly on 3 of 19, the old bake at amp=0 on 2 of 19, and NEITHER is byte-identical anywhere. What section 2c should require is that the undisplaced arm be no further from the flat wall than the shipped bake is -- which v4 satisfies on every column.

- `ba7c2e825b1f` trap **greets.render.mip_is_per_polygon** = 265108 (count) {granularity=whole_frame metric=miplevel scene=greets} w=3/5  [floating] — THE ALBEDO MIP IS A FUNCTION OF THE TESSELLATION, NOT ONLY OF THE CAMERA. FDS/FRUSTRUM/FRUSTRUM.CPP:945 picks the miplevel per CLIPPED POLYGON: under MinSize = XRes*YRes*0.02 pixels (41472 at 1920x1080) one mip is taken for the whole polygon from 0.5*log2(texArea*SizeX*SizeY/pixArea); above it the mipmap-via-subdivision path splits the polygon and each piece gets its own. So subdividing a wall changes which mip its pixels sample even when every new vertex lies exactly on the authored plane: 265108 pixels change mip (0->1 on 225884, 0->2 on 39224) between the 226 authored stone triangles and a 60606-triangle UNDISPLACED lattice of the same planes at greets cam A, and the SHIPPED bake at amplitude 0 shows the same signature. Consequence for any 'byte-identical after retessellation' plan: it cannot hold. Consequence for any A/B whose arms differ in face count: the colour delta is not attributable to the shading change under test until the mip plane is differenced -- the G-buffer packs it in bits 28..31 of the mat32 word, dumpable with FDS_SNAPSHOT_GBUFDUMP and read by tools/v4_p2_planes.py.

Implemented as sin(elev) = dot(d,N)*dinv and measured: it does NOT fix the t=5836 grazing near-wall banding at all - std 0.166 at 0.05 and 0.163 at 0.10 against 0.146 unfixed, versus 0.073 for the plane-DISTANCE form. That wall's error is the ZPage16 depth quantum, a fixed SIZE, not a fixed angle. Generalising from one traced pixel to the whole defect class was the mistake. The distance form is what the flag ships; the angle form is recorded here and in the flag help so it is not re-derived from the same trace.

- `c1c3a8672b39` refutation **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band.mitigation** = 16.8 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_edge_clamp metric=pct_increase_in_mean_abs_border_error pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — CLAMP-TO-EDGE SAMPLING DOES NOT FIX THE GTAO BORDER BAND HERE - IT OVERSHOOTS. --ssao_gtao_edge_clamp replaces the off-frame `continue` (open horizon) with a clamp of the sample coordinate into the frame. Measured against the synthetic border (--ssao_edge_inset=256, where the un-truncated answer exists): inside 256 px of that border the un-mitigated error is +0.0860 AO (too bright) and with edge_clamp it is -0.0361 (too DARK); mean |error| rises 0.0860 -> 0.1004, i.e. the mitigation makes it 16.8 % WORSE in absolute terms. Mechanism: a whole run of march steps collapses onto one border pixel, which reads as a solid occluder at a fixed angle. It is at least strictly localised - the interior delta is exactly 0.0000 - so it costs nothing outside the band. The other two arms priced at the same pose: --ssao_gtao_edge_fade=256 lifts the band +0.1032 (it simply DISCARDS the AO there, interior 0.0000); --ssao_gtao_srad_max=1024 moves the INTERIOR too (+0.0288) because the same 4 steps then span 1024 px and lose near-contact occlusion (meanAO 0.7796 -> 0.8111).
- `a0fefb201622` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.resolution_dependence** = 12.6 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pp_swing_in_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — GTAO AO IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE SCENE - it moves with the pixel grid, and the hemisphere sampler does not. Same camera, same geometry, same flags, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed: GTAO ao<0.9 = 73.7 % at 1280x720, 66.5 % at 1920x1080, 61.1 % at 2560x1440 (meanAO 0.7732 / 0.7795 / 0.7928) - a 12.6-POINT swing from resolution alone. The --no-ssao_gtao hemisphere control at the same two of those resolutions gives 44.4 % and 44.3 % (meanAO 0.8778 / 0.8776), i.e. FLAT. Mechanism: the 256-px cap binds, so the world AO radius is cap*viewZ/FOVX, and FOVX is proportional to XRes - the radius therefore scales with depth AND inversely with horizontal resolution. This is the view-dependent shading Gil-Ad reported, and it is why his ladder cleared --no-ssao_gtao.

- `9f6575e68c03` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.round_truncation** = 0.0044 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_round_fix metric=rms_ao_delta_of_the_fix pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — REAL BUG, TOP/LEFT-SIGNED, SECOND ORDER. The GTAO march computed its sample pixel as `px + int(off + 0.5f)`, and C truncation toward zero makes that round-half-up ONLY for off >= -0.5; every negative offset lands ONE PIXEL SHORT. It does not average out over the rotations, because buildSliceTrig builds phi = (s + jit)*(PI/slices) with jit in [0,1], so phi never leaves [0,PI) and sin(phi) - the y component of every slice direction at all 16 rotations - is non-negative: every UP-going sample landed 1 px low and every LEFT-going sample 1 px right, while down/right were exact. Fixed by rounding to nearest (--ssao_gtao_round_fix, default ON, one instruction CHEAPER). Delta at cam A: rms 0.0044 AO, max 0.0908, 3.39 % of px move >0.01; asymmetric in the predicted direction - top 256-px band mean -0.00185 (6.82 % of px >0.01) vs bottom +0.00186 (1.12 %), left -0.00140 (4.24 %) vs right -0.00068 (0.03 %).
- `bfd122c99a15` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.srad_clamp** = 99.99 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pct_covered_px_where_cap_binds pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — The GTAO march hard 256-px screen-radius cap (FDS/RENDER/DeferredSSAO.cpp#gtaoCell / #gtaoRow8: srad = ssao_radius*FOVX/z clamped to [2,256]) BINDS on 92-100 % of covered pixels at the greets review poses, so the AO scale in this scene is set by the cap, not by --ssao_radius. From the --ssao_dump view-Z plane at 1920x1080 (FOVX=1728, --ssao_radius=4, the zfloor inert in greets) the unclamped srad median is 894 px at t=5965, 1034 at t=5743 and 9333 at t=6097, and the cap binds on 99.99 / 92.12 / 100.00 % of covered pixels.

- **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.frame_ms** — 2 current records, by arm/flag/metric; no single current value  (+1 superseded)
    - `846db4d1125f` arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta: 0.12 (pct_delta) [unverified_at_tip] — COST-NEUTRAL, and the SSAO wave itself is slightly CHEAPER. greets t=5743, 1920x1080, --deferred_prof=5 --bench iters=40, 11 INTERLEAVED runs per arm on ONE binary, wall_min: renderFrame 49.792 (uniform/256) vs 49.853 (geometric/1024), +0.061 ms = +0.12 %, inside the +/-1.5-2 % placement floor; the ssao wave 7.140 -> 6.853 (-4.02 %) and ssao-march 5.007 -> 4.728 (-5.57 %), with the medians agreeing in sign (renderFrame median is LOWER for the geometric arm, 50.336 vs 51.482). Mechanism for the march being cheaper rather than dearer: the step count is unchanged, but a ~894-px march puts more samples OFF-FRAME, and the off-frame path skips the depth gather, both gtaoAcos calls and the bitmask build - and a whole 8-lane batch with no live sample takes the `if (!any) continue` exit. The per-cell ladder constants cost two quadratic minimax evaluations, not libm.
    - `aa1c1a3982bc` arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-stepdist1-srad1024-guards-bias02-displace flag=ssao_gtao_bias metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta_vs_preround_default: 0.27 (pct_delta) — 11 INTERLEAVED --bench pairs on ONE binary (arms differ only by flags: the OLD arm is --ssao_gtao_step_dist=0 --ssao_gtao_srad_max=256 --no-ssao_gtao_round_fix, proved byte-exact against the pre-round build), iters=40, 1920x1080 forced, --deferred_prof=5 --profiler=0, dummy drivers, wall_min. THE SHIPPED FLIP'S renderFrame COST AT GREETS IS NOT RESOLVABLE ABOVE THE PLACEMENT FLOOR, and the +1.40 % this supersedes is not reproduced. renderFrame 46.240 -> 46.367 ms (+0.127 ms, +0.27 %) against a +/-1.5-2 % floor; a SECOND independent 11-round battery on the same binary minutes earlier gave 46.183 -> 45.888 (-0.64 %), i.e. the two batteries straddle zero, which is what 'inside the floor' means. The ROW numbers are stable and DO clear their floor: ssao 6.914 -> 7.169 (+3.69 %), ssao-march 4.838 -> 5.087 (+5.15 %), ssao-apply +0.06 %, ssao-blur +1.15 % - and those reproduce the superseded record's own row figures (+3.64 % / +5.47 %) to within a tenth of a point. So the guards' per-row cost is confirmed; what is corrected is the claim that it shows up as +1.4 % of the frame. Mechanism unchanged: the SIMD march MASKS rejected samples rather than branching around them, so a rejected sample costs its full arithmetic plus the guard, and the guards give back the off-frame early-out that made the unguarded geometric arm look free.
- `e279eac2ea39` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.near_step_artifacts** = 0.03 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024 flag=ssao_gtao_thick_clamp metric=pct_fully_black_px_in_the_saw_tooth_column pose=t5726 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — BOTH OF HIS ARTIFACTS ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS NOT A DEPTH-DISCONTINUITY BUG. Traced at t=5726 px(1129,225): the black saw-tooth column is NOT the near wall occluding the far background - those samples are correctly rejected (OUT-OF-RANGE, |d| = 31.5 against a 4 u radius). The black pixels are ON THE NEAR WALL, grazing, just inside its own silhouette, occluding THEMSELVES. Two guards were missing because the uniform ladder put the first sample 48-192 px out and never needed them. (a) THICKNESS: the bitmask back face is d - V*thickness, a flat world constant, so when |d| is much smaller than thickness it lands BEHIND the receiver and acos(dot(b,V)) saturates at PI - in the trace one sample at |d| = 0.066 set 11 of 32 sectors and one at 0.285 set THIRTY, and the saturation vanishes at the first step whose |d| (1.274) exceeds thickness (1.0). Fixed by --ssao_gtao_thick_clamp: back face bounded by min(thickness,|d|). (b) SELF-OCCLUSION: --ssao_bias is read ONLY by the hemisphere sampler, so GTAO shipped with no bias at all; at ~5 px the ZPage16 quantum (0.0025 u) and the half-res cell offset become a horizon at grazing incidence. Fixed by --ssao_gtao_bias (plane distance, AUTO 0.05 u). RESULTS at 1512x848 on his exact arm: t=5726 column fully-black fraction 32.90 % -> 0.03 % (std 0.343 -> 0.216); t=5836 column 8.23 % -> 0.00 % (std 0.280 -> 0.148, the shipped uniform arm is 0.147); t=5836 near-wall banding std 0.1462 -> 0.0725, BELOW the shipped uniform arm's 0.0796. Isolation: bias alone leaves 11.58 %/1.36 % black (the clamp owns the columns), clamp alone leaves the banding at std 0.139 (the bias owns the banding). --ssao_downscale=1 changes neither (37.59 %/9.86 % black), so the bilateral upsample is NOT implicated - that hypothesis is refuted.
- `6a6ad4f75dda` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.pins** = "re-baselined-on-his-ruling" (state) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024-guards-bias02 flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=acceptance_pin_delta scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE PINS ARE RE-BASELINED - the state both superseded records were waiting on. - **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.resolution** — 2 current records, by arm/metric; no single current value
    - `6c0f5448991c` arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace metric=pp_spread_of_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560_at_cap4096: 0.47 (share_pct) [unverified_at_tip] — THE STRUCTURAL FIX WORKS: front-loaded march steps plus a cap large enough to stop binding collapse GTAO's resolution dependence to the hemisphere sampler's level. Same camera, same geometry, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed; ao<0.9 spread over 1280x720 / 1920x1080 / 2560x1440. Pose A: uniform+cap256 12.4 pp -> geometric+cap512 3.5 -> +cap1024 3.3 -> +cap4096 (cap binds 0.00 %) 0.47 pp, against the hemisphere control's 0.2 pp. Pose B: 8.6 -> 5.1 -> 1.7 -> 0.21 pp, hemisphere 0.5. meanAO at pose A goes 0.7729/0.7793/0.7925 (spread 0.0196) to 0.7780/0.7800/0.7821 (spread 0.0041). Pose C does NOT converge (5.9 -> 5.2 pp at cap 1024, 5.6 at cap 4096) and it is not a failure of the lever: at view-Z median 0.74 the authored 4-unit radius subtends more than the whole frame, the cap still binds on 84-99 % of pixels, and no screen-space method can carry that radius there. GEOMETRIC SPACING IS WHAT MAKES THE RAISED CAP USABLE: at cap 1024 the four steps land at 5.1/26.1/133.2/681.0 px instead of uniform's 192/448/704/960, and uniform at the same cap costs +0.0290 AO of interior contact occlusion where geometric costs +0.0152.

- **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.causal_ab** — 2 current records, by (no distinguishing scope); no single current value  (+1 superseded)
    - `d585fec1a494` : "83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281" (snapshot_md5) [unverified_at_tip] — THE M5 CEILING REPRODUCED ON A CORRECT MACHINE, AND THE GUARD RESTORES IT. greets cam A t=5965, 1920x1080, dev box, clean Release at 81c4db52. --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 stamps id+N into the shadow map's id half leaving the DEPTH half untouched, so every receiver's own texels carry a non-matching id at ZERO depth separation - the M5's ceiling state, frame-wide. THIS RECORD'S VALUE IS THE UNGUARDED REPRO SNAPSHOT md5, 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281, whose ceiling band (rows 5..40, cols 600..1300) reads 82.8/63.4/47.6 against the M5's measured 85.3/65.8/47.8. The other three arms of the same battery: shipping d92cb6f5eb19da4301588ae83af6a56e = 85.2/133.5/139.0; idshift + --shadow_coplanar_guard 8dbbafb77629598e5a3e2b1bfaae9e1a = 85.1/133.8/139.3, i.e. with EVERY identity rescue in the scene destroyed the guard alone carries the frame back to within 0.3/255 of the shipping look; idshift + guard + --shadow_coplanar_guard_onesided bc7a68cc2e5eeec1ca4ab8ef350b5658, which is --no-shadow_polyid BYTE FOR BYTE (so requiring the depth test to agree degenerates to depth mode and the SYMMETRIC band is the actual guard). The raster-noise lever --shadow_coplanar_flip=K (last writer wins within K quanta) is much weaker: K=1 moves 3 px, K<=8 moves at most 406 px in one 250x160 box and NEVER the ceiling band - exact and near-exact ownership ties are rare in these maps, so tie-flipping alone cannot be the whole M5 story; the M5 needs the id half itself to differ, which its pervasive map divergence supplies.
    - `efbf02b4143e` : "83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281" (snapshot_md5) [unverified_at_tip] — recheck of d585fec1a494: THE M5 CEILING REPRODUCED ON A CORRECT MACHINE, AND THE GUARD RESTORES IT. greets cam A t=5965, 1920x1080, dev box, clean Release at 81c4db52. --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 stamps id+N into the shadow map's id half leaving the DEPTH half untouched, so every receiver's own texels carry a non-matching id at ZERO depth separation - the M5's ceiling state, frame-wide. The raster-noise lever --shadow_coplanar_flip=K (last writer wins within K quanta) is much weaker: K=1 moves 3 px, K<=8 moves at most 406 px in one 250x160 box and NEVER the ceiling band - exact and near-exact ownership ties are rare in these maps, so tie-flipping alone cannot be the whole M5 story; the M5 needs the id half itself to differ, which its pervasive map divergence supplies. — 'cd Runtime && rm -rf /tmp/sgrc && SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy FDS_GREETS_CAM="22.5084476,3.87992334,-61.8882256,-0.829246342,-0.20816116,0.518670499" ./DEMO --deferred --hdr --hdr-linear --texture-filter=2 --ssao --ssao-gtao --greets-displace --force_xres=1920 --force_yres=1080 --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 --snapshot=greets@t=5965 --out=/tmp/sgrc >/dev/null 2>&1 && md5 -q /tmp/sgrc/greets_t005965_color.ppm' -> 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281 (was 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281, agrees)
- `79bedaa08607` refutation **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.constant_quanta** = 512 (count) {arm=idshift_guard_constN metric=guard_band_quanta pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — REFUTED: a SMALL CONSTANT tolerance cannot be the co-planar guard's band. The brief proposed N = 1-2 shadow-map depth quanta. Measured at greets cam A t=5965 under the --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 repro, the ceiling band stays at the fully-shadowed 82.8/63.4/47.6 for N = 2, 8, 64 AND 512, and only at N = 4096 does it start to recover (83.6/103.4/104.6). VALUE = 512, the largest constant band measured that rescues NOTHING. MECHANISM: the deltas are SLOPE-sized, not quantum-sized - the receiver's depth is sampled at the PIXEL's exact position while the stored depth is the occluder's depth at the TEXEL it won, so on a slanted surface (texel footprint x depth slope) is thousands of quanta even when the two polygons are exactly co-planar. This is the same quantity --shadow_slope_bias (1024, reaching 4096 at the 0.2 N.L clamp) exists to cover in depth mode. The guard's default band is therefore --shadow_coplanar_guard_quanta = -1 = the DEPTH-MODE BIAS for that very pixel (shadow_bias + the slope-scaled term), which is narrow (512) where the surface faces the light and wide (up to 4608) where it grazes. THE TRADE a larger N buys: a real occluder standing within N quanta of the receiver loses its last N quanta of contact shadow.
- `ad6b638504d1` measurement **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.on_arm_cost** = 19.92 (share_pct) {arm=guard_on metric=px_changed_vs_off pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — WHAT THE FLAG-ON ARM COSTS ON THE DEV BOX, MEASURED not assumed. tools/render_gate.sh under FDS_SHADOW_COPLANAR_GUARD=1 is still 4/4 PASS with all four rows BYTE-IDENTICAL - those scenes have no co-planar shadow-group conflict. greets does, and it moves a lot. Same recipe, OFF -> ON: cam A t=5965 413,078 px differ (VALUE = 19.92%% of frame), 124,786 px >8, max delta 136, signed mean +4.63, 412,590 brighter vs 477 darker; default-camera t=1588 789,885 px (38.09%%), 155,842 >8, max 120, mean +3.69; t=2845 558,382 px (26.93%%), 292,069 >8, max 140, mean +12.07; t=5965 304,548 px (14.69%%), 99,823 >8, max 138, mean +4.75. DIRECTION: >=99.6%% of every changed pixel is BRIGHTER - the guard removes shadow and never adds any, which is what it is built to do, but on greets it removes a great deal. Whether that is wrongly-cast self-shadow (correct) or real contact shadow (a look regression) is HIS eye's call, not these counters'. PERF, single run --deferred_prof=1 at cam A: DeferredLighting-call 22.133 -> 24.697 ms (+2.56 ms, +11.6%%), lighting-w1 17.503 -> 20.002 ms (+2.50 ms), lighting-w2 2.549 -> 2.645 ms; most of it is the disarmed --deferred_cube_prepass, whose cached tail is called with lz=0 and so cannot serve the guard. Default stays OFF.

### perf
- `8f04128dc038` law **perf.floor** = 2.0 (pct_delta) {granularity=kernel_row metric=delta_pct} w=5/5  [floating] — a single kernel row's between-binary delta carries a +-1.5-2 % code-placement floor: 24 bytes of inert padding moved greets lighting-w1 by 1.21 %, and an arm with the parent's exact 5359-instruction kernel still carried +1.08 % (00w).
- `d5f079501e3c` law **perf.law.L1_fanout_threshold** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — A fan-out pays only above a work-per-dispatch threshold of ~tens of microseconds; under ~100 us the round trip is the cost (city glass -24 %, RTT cone pass +139 %, mirror-mask clear +33 %). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `95a401b2efdd` law **perf.law.L2_scalar_ilp** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — An 8-iteration independent scalar loop is not automatically improved by vectorising it: the scalar form already extracts ILP; price it in cycles, never in instructions (SSAO gather -9.8 % instr, +2.5 % cycles). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `2a4700fe068d` law **perf.law.L3_register_pressure** = "holds" (state) {granularity=kernel_row} w=5/5  [floating] — Register pressure beats op count in the cone and lighting kernels: C6 removed 10 vector-ALU ops and paid 29 spills, +5.42 % cycles; one __m256 is two of arm64's 32 v-registers. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `35721b7adc41` law **perf.law.L4_issue_bound** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — Once a kernel is issue-bound instruction counts stop predicting time; the cone pass at ~81 % of NEON issue converts instructions to wall, lighting-w1 does not (-2.0 % instr -> +0.7..1.4 % cycles). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `3c27392e58f7` law **perf.law.L5_cores_triage** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating] — cores = Gcyc / clock / wall finds both the wins and the refutations: a row far below the worker count is serial or barrier-bound, one at the worker count is compute-bound. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `0236fb850a7c` law **perf.law.L6_fewer_pairs** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — The only lever with real headroom on a saturated per-pixel row is FEWER PAIRS: micro-optimising the body is exhausted (cone pass -52.6 % by range cull; greets lighting-w1 8.40 lights/px enter, 2.06-3.40 accumulate). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `e55e19b2c7a2` law **perf.law.L7_share_source** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — Bit-exactness is won by SHARING SOURCE, not transcribing: the froxel reflection leg went 8-wide bit-exact first build by lifting the leg into one static inline helper both paths call. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `4a4c2a5695d3` law **perf.law.L8_per_tile_decidable** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating] — Anything that runs per-pixel and is decidable per-tile or per-light is the first place to look, and the guard usually already exists - check before proposing it. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `6761cfcf6bae` law **perf.law.L9_runtime_predicate** = "holds" (state) {row=lighting-w1 scene=greets} w=5/5  [floating] — In a kernel at its register-allocation limit a new runtime predicate costs more than the work it removes: a never-taken skip inside the cube tap cost +12.4 %, a runtime bool hatch +4.3 % with the flag OFF; the cube tap only gets cheaper by being CALLED less. - `48a5fe799eae` trap **perf.trap.T14_instruments_need_frames** = "trap" (state) {tool=census} w=1/5  [floating] — Instruments need the frames they were built for: both shadow censuses report every 8 main-view frames and the cone/fog censuses report the PREVIOUS frame; a one-pose snapshot prints nothing. - `8edd0370e6fc` trap **perf.trap.T1_green_pins_not_exercised** = "trap" (state) {tool=snapshot} w=1/5  [floating] — A green 13/13 does not mean a path was exercised: every pin is a one-tick --snapshot and default-ON paths that switch on from tick 2 execute zero times; a binary computing no city water fog passed 12/12 pins and failed 5/7 warm rows. Use tools/warm_gate.sh. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `f13a58dcf4e6` trap **perf.trap.T1b_cold_census_misprices** = "trap" (state) {tool=census} w=1/5  [floating] — A cold one-tick census mis-prices the backlog: the froxel composite's scalar half was on file at 0.72 % of renderFrame (cold, 1512x848) and is 5.15 % warm at 1920x1080, 7x low. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `94f29f379cf6` trap **perf.trap.T2_stale_shares** = "trap" (state) {scene=greets} w=1/5  [floating] — Stale shares get stale: re-take the measurement before sizing a plan off it (the 36.6 % cube-tap share stood for eleven rounds; all shadow work is 16-28 %). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `58dd332647a8` trap **perf.trap.T3_hwread_process_wide** = "trap" (state) {tool=TailProf} w=1/5  [floating] — TailProf::hwRead() is proc_pid_rusage(getpid()) - PROCESS-WIDE; a nested scope inside a threaded pass measures every thread. Split threaded sub-scopes by ablation differencing, never by nesting a timer. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `d5d5ac74d34d` trap **perf.trap.T4_chdir_asset_root** = "trap" (state) {tool=DEMO} w=1/5  [floating] — ChdirToAssetRoot makes a binary's FILE LOCATION select its assets: DEMO chdirs to its own directory, so a worktree binary always renders that worktree's assets. Three false alarms traced to this; gate with --no-chdir_assets. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `76b2bfb5c3e4` trap **perf.trap.T5_chase_pose_sequence** = "trap" (state) {scene=chase tool=snapshot} w=1/5  [floating] — chase pins are POSE-SEQUENCE dependent: t=800 alone differs from t=800 third of five by 434 591 px (20.96 %, max 5); only the first pose of a process is comparable across recipes. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `69fbcb8b6628` trap **perf.trap.T6_pgrep_self_match** = "trap" (state) {tool=pgrep} w=1/5  [floating] — pgrep -f matches your own shell: two agents' box-quiet loops deadlocked on each other's pgrep command lines. Use ps -Ao comm= | grep -c '/DEMO$'. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `e088f40341b3` trap **perf.trap.T7_ssao_dump_inflates** = "trap" (state) {flag=ssao_dump} w=1/5  [floating] — --ssao_dump forces the scalar apply loop and inflates the pass it measures ~3.5x (4.2 -> 13.7-16.0 ms); a correctness instrument, never a perf one. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `e3623046e07e` trap **perf.trap.T8_cmake_install_rpath** = "trap" (state) {tool=cmake} w=1/5  [floating] — cmake --install fails on a pre-existing rpath step (install_name_tool: no LC_RPATH) - that is NOT a failed build; the binary is placed by the POST_BUILD copy. Verify with --no-chdir_assets. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `419d92354f90` trap **perf.trap.T9_warm_gate_zero_frames** = "trap" (state) {tool=warm_gate.sh} w=1/5  [floating] — A row that emitted no frames must not print as a pixel mismatch: warm_gate.sh printed an empty got under FAIL and sent a reader hunting a regression for an hour; it now prints ERROR <row> produced N/M frames. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `5147ddf34ca9` trap **perf.trap.bake_face_array_is_whole_trimesh** = "trap" (state) {scene=greets tool=DisplaceStoneSubdiv} w=1/5  [floating] — DisplaceStoneSubdiv's faces/fIdx hold the WHOLE TriMesh (foreign-material faces appended verbatim), so any pass looping 0..nF0 must test isTargetNew - the inherited tsplit split 530 floor faces from the rooms bake. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from rev-dispfix handoff / SESSION_STATE]
- `3d167a8c2c71` trap **perf.trap.refdiff_raw_is_tracked** = "trap" (state) {scene=greets tool=git} w=1/5  [floating] — docs/img/refdiff/raw holds 120 TRACKED verdict-corpus dumps; do not rm -rf it as scratch (a fork deleted and restored them from HEAD). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from rev-dispfix handoff / SESSION_STATE]
- `c50b39a206fc` trap **perf.trap.tear_battery_absolute_outroot** = "trap" (state) {tool=tear_battery.sh} w=1/5  [floating] — tools/tear_battery.sh needs an ABSOLUTE outroot, or every pose silently renders nothing while printing done. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from rev-dispfix handoff / SESSION_STATE]
- `f4343b294f7e` trap **perf.trap.zsh_flag_string_splitting** = "trap" (state) {tool=zsh} w=1/5  [floating] — zsh does not word-split a variable holding a flag string: use ${=VAR} to split it, and never echo a line of ===. - `36a71fc53e68` measurement **platform.m5.build_type_cross_check** = "3_of_4_rows_o2_invariant_none_match_m5" (state) {state=cross_check_2026_08_30 tool=m5} w=3/5 — Dev-box cross-check of the M5 build-type axis: a RelWithDebInfo (-O2) build with Apple clang 21 reproduces the recorded Release gate hashes on 3 of 4 rows (mirrortest, rttslot, conetest PASS at 4ac809e5/826c09e6/b41894f9) and diverges only on halotest (548473e9 vs 166fa25a). - `d7a3991f56ca` measurement **platform.m5.class1_edge_cracks** = 1 (hole_px) {arm=bare defect=edge_crack pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — A SECOND, LOCAL defect found while sweeping: the same near-clipped greets floor triangle drops isolated 1-pixel HOLES on its own silhouette edge under tiny camera motion. 4 of 72 camera perturbations produced 1 empty texel each and 1 produced 3, always ON the projected edge that runs from the un-clipped vertex to a near-plane vertex (e.g. (1070,1070), which the measured edge (1280,1007.613)->(1036.344,1080) passes through exactly). The base pose has ZERO empty texels frame-wide, so this is a real seam between the floor and whatever abuts it, opened by the same 1/NZP=100 coordinate blow-up, not by a cull.
- `1bef41c539ec` refutation **platform.m5.class1_knifeedge_cull** = 0 (hole_px) {arm=bare defect=missing_polys pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=5/5 — REFUTED at cam A on the dev box: there is NO 1-ULP-margin acceptance test behind the class-1 hole. Instrumented margins for the one face that owns the region (Piramid.lwo:c0 fi=0): mesh bsphere near test lhs 77.82 vs rhs 423.76 (5.4x), far test dz=-158.81 vs 0, left/right 3.078e8 vs 1.655e9 (5.4x), up/down 1.519e8 vs 8.351e8 (5.5x); face backface AP.N-NormProd = -3.8799 against a 0 threshold; VisibilityFlagsAll=0; needle-cull and tile-bbox both take their documented behind-near escape (cover-all box, no reject); clipper CorrectCWOrder area = -1.468e12 (no sign risk); MiplevelClipper iml==iMl==0 in every surviving tile so the split path is never entered. Every margin is 2^21-2^22 ULPs, not 1. Empirically: 72 camera perturbations (each of the 6 FDS_GREETS_CAM components, +/-1e-6 to +/-0.1 world units) never removed the region - only 1-3 single-pixel cracks ON the face silhouette edge in 4 of 72 runs; and 24 seeds of a value-keyed +/-1-ULP fuzz applied to TPos/PX/PY/RZ of every clipper input vertex produced 476-1033 texels of +/-1 z16 speckle and ZERO empty texels in all 24. The class-1 amplifier is NOT in transform/cull/clip acceptance.
- `29932b78bd51` measurement **platform.m5.class1_region_is_one_face** = 1 (count) {arm=bare defect=missing_polys pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — The M5 bare-arm 115,416-texel hole is ONE authored triangle, not thousands: the face-id plane (--face_id_dump) resolves every one of those texels to a single key (Piramid.lwo:c0, face 0, material floor::mirUV, plane N=(0,1,0) d=-0, verts (17.898,0,-49.374)/(17.898,0,-75.913)/(49.374,0,-75.913)). It is heavily near-plane clipped: A view-z=11.12 in front, B=-2.643 and C=-28.74 BEHIND the camera, so the clipper turns it into a 3-gon whose two new vertices sit on NZP=0.01 with screen coords PX=-1726688.62/+1132107.12, PY=514363.34. It survives in exactly 4 of the 30 raster tiles (T1280x1080, T1600x1080, T1920x864, T1920x1080) whose union is the missing bbox.
- `d2c0f282ac33` measurement **platform.m5.class2_ceiling_is_missing_lights** = 3 (count) {arm=displaced defect=ceiling_colour pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — NAMED the ceiling defect: it is three MISSING OMNI LIGHTS, not geometry. All 84,274 pixels with colour delta > 32 belong to Piramid.lwo:c57 / material siling (the stone ceiling, texture TEXTURES/PSILING.JPG - proven by painting that JPEG magenta, which turns the band magenta); z16 is IDENTICAL on 100% of them, matID identical on 100%, the whole packed mat word identical on 51,429 of 51,836 in rows 0-60. Per channel the two machines agree EXACTLY on R (75.1% bit-equal, mean |dR| 0.9) and differ by x2.22 on G and x3.20 on B. Locally skipping exactly the three cyan mech omnis (light seq 114/115/116, colour (0,128,255), ISize 0.75, IRange 30, world pos (11.834,1.920,-56.260)/(10.475,1.905,-56.954)/(10.104,3.107,-54.365)) drops the ceiling band mean from (85.2,133.5,139.0) to (85.2,65.0,46.9) against the M5s (85.3,65.8,47.8) - a match to ~1/255. Those same three lights still reach the REST of the M5 frame (removing them locally moves 1,181,296 px > 8; the M5 differs on only 147,588), so this is a REGION-LOCAL loss of three lights on one surface.
- `23ab524027e5` measurement **platform.m5.class2_ceiling_localised_to_c57** = 57.4 (share_pct) {arm=displaced defect=ceiling_colour pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — The M5 light loss is SPATIALLY LOCALISED, not frame-wide: per-mesh, the three cyan omnis matter on 76-100%% of the pixels of many greets chunks (c43 77%, c50 100%, c82 95%, c42 98%, c51 40%, c74 82%, c90 96%), yet the M5 differs from the dev box on only 0-8%% of every one of those - except Piramid.lwo:c57, where 124,155 of 216,468 pixels (57%) differ. So on the M5 those three lights still reach the whole room and fail only on roughly half of ONE ceiling chunk. The 3-up image shows it: the M5 ceiling is stone-brown on the far band and still cyan on the near band of the same surface, while removing the three lights locally browns the whole band. Whatever the gate is, it is region-scoped inside Render_DeferredLighting with a bit-identical G-buffer underneath (z16 and matID identical on 100%% of the divergent pixels).

- `dad4de8f8962` measurement **platform.m5.polyid_has_no_depth_term** = "identity_only_no_bias" (state) {defect=ceiling_colour scene=greets state=mechanism_2026_08_31 tool=m5} w=1/5  [unverified_at_tip] — MECHANISM CONFIRMED FROM THE CODE (docs/SHADOW_GUARD.md §1): ShadowMode::PolyId decides occlusion by IDENTITY ALONE - `occ += w iff c != 0 && c != receiverId` in CubeShadow_Tail (FDS/FILLERS/ShadowMap.h), with NO depth term and NO bias; DeferredShadowSampling.h passes constBias=0/slopeBias=0 into it on purpose. Depth mode's constBias(512)+slopeBias(<=4096) IS the acne rescue and it is OWNERSHIP-BLIND, so a co-planar occluder reads LIT whoever wrote the texel - which is exactly why --no-shadow_polyid restores the M5 ceiling while the default shadows it. PolyId's only rescue is id equality, so a different-shadow-group co-planar neighbour hard-shadows the receiver with ZERO depth separation from its own plane. Ownership is decided by `enc > z_existing` in ShadowBarry, i.e. by ONE quantum, and the M5's maps diverge on 41/48 static maps. SHARPENING of the parked hypothesis: the id is a MATERIAL/shadow-group id (Material::ShadowMatID, else ID+1), NOT a per-polygon id, so same-group co-planar polygons never fight - and greets allocates 2183 per-plane-cluster shadow groups by design ([PIRAMID-SPLIT] census), which is 2183 chances for the mismatch. The predicate is copied in FOUR places: the cube tail, LightmapBake.cpp SampleStaticCubeAtWorld (the path greets STATIC omnis actually take), the spot tap in DeferredSurfaceKernel.cpp, and the cached tail in CubeTapPrepass.h.

The snapshot VALIDATES the mechanism: his baseline band 85.3/65.8/47.8, guarded 85.5/133.9/139.4 - the predicted ~85/134/139 restored on the real machine, so the co-planar polyId rescue IS the amplifier at t=5965. But flying with the guard he still sees the defect ('doesn't seem to fix'). Unknowns: whether his fly carried the full flag set the snapshot used, and whether what he sees in flight is the same ceiling band or another face of the pervasive divergence (69 of 91 maps differ; the guard only rescues co-planar different-group texels). Follow-up question filed with the discriminating commands.  (+2 superseded)

- `9b1594694cbb` measurement **platform.m5.speckle_mechanism** = 5.6e-05 (world_units) {arm=bare metric=clip_reconstruction_disagreement pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — NAMED the +/-1-quantum z16 speckle amplifier: the near-plane clip at Scene NZP=0.01 multiplies every clipped vertex screen coordinate by iNearZ=100, so a near-straddling face is carried through the 2D clip as coordinates of magnitude 1e6 (measured on the greets floor face: PX=-1726688.62 / +1132107.12, PY=514363.34 at RZ=100 against the un-clipped A at PX=2228.13, RZ=0.0899). The subsequent per-tile Left/Right/Up/Down interpolations then reconstruct the SAME screen point differently per tile: the shared corner (1600,1080) comes out at view z 6.29777861 from tile T1920x1080 and 6.29772234 from tile T1600x1080 - a 5.6e-5 world-unit disagreement, about 100 ULPs, or 0.02 of a z16 quantum at zscale=395.64. That is the same order as the +-1-quantum speckle both machines show (10,376 texels bare / 10,493 displaced), and a +-1-ULP fuzz of the clipper inputs reproduces 476-1033 such texels locally.

- `ccd53bb80c90` trap **tool.cmake.language_cxx_on_mm** = "trap" (state) {tool=cmake} w=1/5  [floating] — set_source_files_properties(... LANGUAGE CXX) on Objective-C++ .mm sources emits an explicit -x c++ since CMake CMP0119 (>=3.20; NEW by default with a fresh 4.x cache), overriding the .mm extension: clang warns argument unused for -ObjC++ and then rejects every @class in the Foundation headers. Bit the M5 (fresh CLT + fresh cache) while the dev box coasted on an old cache. Fix: enable_language(OBJCXX) in the gated subdirectory and drop the LANGUAGE override; -fobjc-arc stays as a per-file option. Verified: GpuBench configures and links on the dev box with the fix.
- `1c446f7c1625` trap **tool.groundwork.amend_orphans_head** = "trap" (state) {tool=groundwork} w=1/5  [floating] — Records auto-capture the repo HEAD at write time. Writing records and then AMENDING (or rebasing away) that commit leaves their head pointing at a commit that is no longer an ancestor of the tip, so the tool treats them as unmerged-branch records: they can neither re-verify anything nor count as at-tip. Happened 2026-08-30: four agreeing render_gate rows written at f02e31b4, commit amended to 5381c68b, the four baselines stayed unverified (36 instead of 32). Rule: write records, then make a NEW commit; never amend/rebase a commit that records were written against without re-filing them.
